268	\subsection{Computational Cost}
269	\label{sec:computation_cost}
270	
271	Table~\ref{tab:runtime} reports per-document inference cost for the full OBD pipeline (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct scorer, NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, 1{,}000 documents, single-document processing without batching).
272	The scorer log-probability pass dominates GPU time ($20.6 \pm 1.9$\,ms/doc); CPU feature extraction adds $23.4 \pm 6.1$\,ms/doc; LightGBM inference is negligible ($0.74 \pm 0.10$\,ms/doc).
273	Total latency is $44.7 \pm 7.7$\,ms/doc (${\sim}$22 docs/sec), making the pipeline practical for corpus-scale filtering without specialized hardware.
274	Batched scoring would reduce the per-document GPU cost further.

294	\subsection{DeBERTa Upper-Bound Comparison}
295	\label{sec:deberta_upperbound}
296	
297	To assess how much of the theoretical discrimination boundary the 15-feature OBD classifier captures, we fine-tune DeBERTa-v3-base on raw text as an upper-bound probe.
298	Table~\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd} compares $\Kempirical$ across three domains.
299	DeBERTa matches OBD on C4 and Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) but extends $\Kempirical$ to~4 on arXiv (3v4 $\auc = 0.624$, CI $[0.613, 0.635]$), one depth beyond the 15-feature boundary.
300	The gap quantifies the cost of generator-agnostic features: one depth level on structured text, zero on general web text.
301	
302	\begin{table}[t]
303	\centering
304	\caption{$\Kempirical$ comparison: 15-feature OBD vs.\ DeBERTa-v3-base (Qwen-1.5B generation, $\theta{=}0.60$). DeBERTa extends $\Kempirical$ by one depth on arXiv but not on C4 or Code.}
305	\label{tab:deberta_vs_obd}
306	\footnotesize
307	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
308	\toprule
309	& \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{OBD (15D)}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{DeBERTa}} \\
310	\cmidrule(lr){2-3} \cmidrule(lr){4-5}
311	\textbf{Domain} & $\Kempirical$ & boundary $\auc$ & $\Kempirical$ & boundary $\auc$ \\
312	\midrule
313	C4      & 2 & 0.626~(1v2) & 2 & 0.705~(1v2) \\
314	arXiv   & 3 & 0.643~(2v3) & 4 & 0.624~(3v4) \\
315	Code    & 2 & 0.738~(1v2) & 2 & 0.808~(1v2) \\
316	\bottomrule
317	\end{tabular}
318	\end{table}
319	
320	\section{Use of AI Assistants}
321	\label{app:ai-assistants}
322	
323	We used Claude (Anthropic) as an AI coding and writing assistant
324	throughout this work. Specifically:
325	\begin{itemize}
326	    \item \textbf{Code:} drafting experiment scripts, data-processing
327	    pipelines, and analysis utilities; debugging and refactoring.
328	    \item \textbf{Writing:} drafting and revising paper sections,
329	    polishing prose, and reformatting \LaTeX{}.
330	    \item \textbf{Literature:} summarizing related papers to inform
331	    the related-work section.
332	\end{itemize}
333

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "\\section{Use of AI Assistants}\n\\label{app:ai-assistants}",
  "new_string": "\\subsection{Cross-Scale Transfer: Gemma-2-27B}\n\\label{sec:gemma27b_transfer}\n\nTo test whether depth features generalize across model scales, we extend the cross-model transfer matrix (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}) to include Gemma-2-27B (C4 continuation, Qwen-1.5B scorer).\nTable~\\ref{tab:gemma27b_transfer} reports accuracy retention rates for the 5$\\times$5 matrix.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-scale transfer retention (\\%) between Gemma-2-27B and 1B--8B models. Retention $=$ cross-model accuracy / in-domain accuracy $\\times$ 100. The 27B$\\to$small direction retains $>$100\\% on average, indicating that classifiers trained on 27B-generated text capture a universal feature superset; the reverse direction (small$\\to$27B) retains only 76.5\\%.}\n\\label{tab:gemma27b_transfer}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Train $\\backslash$ Test} & \\textbf{Gemma-27B} & \\textbf{LLaMA-8B} & \\textbf{Qwen-1.5B} & \\textbf{Pythia-1.4B} & \\textbf{OLMo-1B} & \\textbf{Avg retention} \\\\\n\\midrule\nGemma-27B   & --- & 103.6\\% & 107.2\\% & 103.5\\% & 93.1\\% & 101.3\\% \\\\\nLLaMA-8B    & 92.1\\% & --- & & & & \\\\\nQwen-1.5B   & 71.3\\% & & --- & & & \\\\\nPythia-1.4B  & 80.9\\% & & & --- & & \\\\\nOLMo-1B     & 77.3\\% & & & & --- & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{l}{Small$\\to$27B avg} & \\multicolumn{5}{r}{76.5\\%} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe asymmetry is interpretable: Gemma-2-27B generates more human-like text ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, weaker depth signal), so a classifier trained on its subtle features easily transfers to smaller models with stronger signals.\nConversely, classifiers trained on coarser small-model features miss the fine-grained patterns needed to discriminate 27B-generated depths.\nThis confirms that depth features are text-intrinsic and scale-modulated rather than model-specific artifacts.\n\n\\section{Use of AI Assistants}\n\\label{app:ai-assistants}"
}
```

          "+\\multicolumn{2}{l}{Small$\\to$27B avg} & \\multicolumn{5}{r}{76.5\\%} \\\\",
          "+\\bottomrule",
          "+\\end{tabular}}",
          "+\\end{table}",
          "+",
          "+The asymmetry is interpretable: Gemma-2-27B generates more human-like text ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, weaker depth signal), so a classifier trained on its subtle features easily transfers to smaller models with stronger signals.",
          "+Conversely, classifiers trained on coarser small-model features miss the fine-grained patterns needed to discriminate 27B-generated depths.",
          "+This confirms that depth features are text-intrinsic and scale-modulated rather than model-specific artifacts.",
          "+",
          " \\section{Use of AI Assistants}",
          " \\label{app:ai-assistants}",
          " "
        ]
      }
    ],

          " Wikipedia yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.619$, 2v3 $\\auc = 0.550$; Table~\\ref{tab:domain_kstar}), matching the C4 baseline.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nOne-wash laundering refers to this concentrated signal loss at the 0v1 boundary: while depth-1 remains marginally distinguishable from depth-2 (1v2 $\\auc$ 0.61--0.70, the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ finding), the dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\\to}1$, and beyond depth~2 no further signal is exploitable.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nOne-wash laundering is distinct from model collapse~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse}: model collapse describes cumulative quality degradation across generations, while one-wash laundering describes the erasure of statistical fingerprints distinguishing adjacent generations; text quality may continue degrading even after the features separating generation $k$ from $k{+}1$ become indistinguishable.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$, while Pythia yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ and OLMo yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 1$, consistent with its 15D boundary (Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).\nThe 15D features trade one depth level of sensitivity for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\\Kempirical{=}2$), making them the preferred operational choice.\nThe gap between $\\Kempirical$ and $\\Ktheory$ remains open; a DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe yields $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = \\Kempirical^{\\text{OBD}} = 2$ on C4 and Code but $\\Kempirical^{\\text{DeBERTa}} = 4$ on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).\nFeature rankings are scorer-independent ($\\rho > 0.90$), but self-scoring inflates boundary AUC (OLMo: $+$11.5\\,pp, Pythia: $+$4.7\\,pp); cross-scoring provides a conservative estimate for deployment against unknown generators (Table~\\ref{tab:runtime}; length confound ruled out in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nWeb text (C4, Wikipedia) and Code (Python) consistently yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four independent architecture families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), as formulaic structure (citations, math notation, section conventions) creates larger per-generation distributional shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4).\nThis elevation is not driven by \\LaTeX{} syntax: stripping all markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (2v3 $\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$); Code (Python), despite rigid syntax, yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, confirming that the elevation reflects academic conventions rather than structural complexity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments across three families (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, Qwen-1.5B; LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (3v4 $\\auc = 0.68$--$0.80$); extending Pythia and Qwen to depth~7 sustains $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (6v7 $\\auc = 0.634$--$0.641 > \\theta$), far exceeding the prompt-chain boundary because each retrain generation also modifies model parameters, introducing cumulative adaptation artifacts absent from text-only recycling.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation, the operationally relevant quantity for data curation pipelines; our conclusions do not directly transfer to retrain-chain (model collapse) settings.\n\n\\paragraph{Decoding strategy as confound.}\nGreedy decoding inflates depth separability: Pythia-1.4B greedy 1v2 $\\auc$ reaches $0.884$ versus $0.617$--$0.727$ for stochastic strategies ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ in all cases), driven by repetition collapse ($d_0{=}0.01 \\to d_1{=}0.72$ 4-gram repeat ratio; Table~\\ref{tab:s10_pythia_decoding}).\n\n",

I need to save about half a line more. Let me try one of these approaches:

But combining them into one long sentence with multiple clauses feels awkward. Let me see if I can tighten the wording instead—maybe I can trim a few words from earlier in the conclusion to buy myself some space, or find a more concise way to express the final thought about future work. ```text
17	Our contributions:
18	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
19	  \item \textbf{Measuring the discrimination boundary}: $\Kempirical \leq 2$ across all 10 model configurations (6 architecture families at 1B--8B scale) and 4 decoding strategies; $\Kempirical{=}2$ for 6/10 spanning three families ($p < 0.001$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
20	  Beyond two generation passes, web text becomes statistically indistinguishable across deeper generations; this finding establishes binary sufficiency by measurement, reveals domain-dependent variation (arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$), and quantifies boundary sensitivity to threshold, scorer, and scale.
21	  Both supervised OBD and zero-shot LLMs fail beyond $\Kempirical$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$; Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
22	  \item \textbf{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$}: general web text (C4, Wikipedia) and code yield $\Kempirical{\leq}2$, while structured academic text (arXiv) yields $\Kempirical{=}3$, consistent across model families (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
23	  \item \textbf{Generator-agnostic cross-model transfer} (within continuation mode): 15 statistical features enable 80--92\% accuracy retention with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown continuation-mode generators without retraining; $\Kempirical$ agreement holds across scorers for strong-signal configurations (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
24	\end{enumerate}
25	
26	\noindent As a consistency check under extreme (100\%) contamination, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts the strategy ordering: binary curation minimizes MMLU degradation ($0.5941$ vs.\ base $0.6018$); at realistic ratios ($\leq$50\%), all strategies converge (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
```

64	
65	% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
66	\section*{Limitations}
67	
68	Our study has four main limitations.
69	(1)~\emph{Scope}: we evaluate models up to 8B parameters across 6 architecture families, English text only (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv, Code), and prompt-chain generation; a single 27B sanity check (Gemma-2-27B, Section~\ref{sec:extended_neural_probe}) yields $\Kempirical{=}1$, but systematic evaluation beyond 8B remains limited, and retrain-chain dynamics may yield different boundaries.
70	Our model sample is unbalanced: Qwen accounts for 4 of 10 configurations, and we do not evaluate RLHF- or DPO-aligned models, whose generation distributions may differ from the base and instruction-tuned models tested here.
71	(2)~\emph{Feature set and transfer scope}: the 15 statistical features provide a lower bound on $\Ktheory$; richer representations (e.g., neural probes) can extend $\Kempirical$ by one additional depth for some architectures.
72	Cross-model transfer (80--92\%) is validated only across continuation-mode generators; rewrite-to-continuation transfer remains untested.
73	(3)~\emph{Threshold sensitivity}: $\Kempirical$ varies with the decision threshold $\theta$, ranging from 2--3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$; three of ten configurations are marginal at our reference $\theta{=}0.60$.
74	(4)~\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with three model families (with bootstrap CI support) on a single structured domain; broader domain coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes.
75	
76	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
77	\section*{Ethics Statement}
78	
```

    "oldString": "(4)~\\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with three model families (with bootstrap CI support) on a single structured domain; broader domain coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes.",
    "newString": "(4)~\\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with four model families (with bootstrap CI support) on a single structured domain; broader domain coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes.",
    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main — ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{colortbl}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage[section]{placeins}\n\\raggedbottom\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*_{\\text{empirical}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure a lower bound on the generational discrimination boundary, $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable).\nUnder prompt-chain generation across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--8B), 4 source domains, and 4 decoding strategies, $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text configurations ($\\theta{=}0.60$); structured academic text (arXiv) reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four independent architecture families.\nBeyond two generation passes, web text becomes statistically indistinguishable across deeper generations, a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect that concentrates the dominant exploitable signal at the human-vs.-machine boundary.\nAcross continuation-mode generators, generator-agnostic statistical features enable 80--92\\% cross-model transfer with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\\rho > 0.90$), supporting depth estimation on corpora from unknown generators without retraining.\nUnder extreme contamination (100\\% synthetic), $\\Kempirical$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives; at realistic ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), strategy differences remain below 0.3\\,pp.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has four main limitations.\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: we evaluate models up to 8B parameters across 6 architecture families, English text only (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv, Code), and prompt-chain generation; a single 27B sanity check (Gemma-2-27B, Section~\\ref{sec:extended_neural_probe}) yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$, but systematic evaluation beyond 8B remains limited, and retrain-chain dynamics may yield different boundaries.\nOur model sample is unbalanced: Qwen accounts for 4 of 10 configurations, and we do not evaluate RLHF- or DPO-aligned models, whose generation distributions may differ from the base and instruction-tuned models tested here.\n(2)~\\emph{Feature set and transfer scope}: the 15 statistical features provide a lower bound on $\\Ktheory$; richer representations (e.g., neural probes) can extend $\\Kempirical$ by one additional depth for some architectures.\nCross-model transfer (80--92\\%) is validated only across continuation-mode generators; rewrite-to-continuation transfer remains untested.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold sensitivity}: $\\Kempirical$ varies with the decision threshold $\\theta$, ranging from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; three of ten configurations are marginal at our reference $\\theta{=}0.60$.\n(4)~\\emph{Domain coverage}: arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is established with three model families (with bootstrap CI support) on a single structured domain; broader domain coverage is needed to confirm this elevation generalizes.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use the publicly available C4 corpus and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\clearpage\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

1	\section{Extended Discussion}
2	\label{sec:extended_discussion}
3	
4	\subsection{Theoretical Implications of $\Kempirical = 2$}
5	
6	The DPI guarantees a finite $\Ktheory$; for general web text, $\Kempirical \leq 2$ across all 10 configurations (1B--8B, 6 families), while structured domains (arXiv) yield $\Kempirical{=}3$, aligning with model collapse convergence~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model, alemohammad2024selfconsuming}.
7	Decoding strategy modestly affects signal strength (1v2 $\auc \in [0.6866, 0.7039]$) but does not shift the boundary.
8	
9	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer Implications}
10	
11	Cross-model classification retains 80--92\% accuracy with scorer-invariant feature rankings ($\rho > 0.90$), enabling single-classifier deployment across generators.
12	Absolute discriminability at the $\Kempirical$ boundary is scorer-sensitive: alternative scorers reduce Qwen-continuation $\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, while rewrite-mode $\Kempirical = 2$ is preserved.
13	Model family similarity modulates transfer (Pythia$\leftrightarrow$OLMo: 89--92\% vs.\ Qwen-cont$\leftrightarrow$OLMo: 80--85\%).
14	
15	\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}

New experiments to integrate:

1. **arxiv_multi_source** (DONE) — Multi-source arXiv (Pythia+OLMo+Qwen combined), K*=3, 2v3 AUC=0.6436. This confirms domain ceiling=3 is model-intrinsic. Since the paper already says "four families" for arXiv K*=3, this multi-source experiment provides additional evidence. It should go in the appendix as supplementary evidence.

2. **wiki_domain_llama** (DONE) — LLaMA-8B Wiki K*=1, 1v2 AUC=0.595 (<0.60). 

This was already captured in the bootstrap table, so no new changes needed there. The arxiv_multi_source result is the key one to add to the appendix since mixing multiple generators still produces K*=3 with the same AUC, which reinforces that the domain determines the ceiling regardless of which models generate the outputs.

3. Remaining pending: code_domain_olmo, gemma27b_filtering, OLMo arXiv/C4 cross-scorers

For `arxiv_multi_source`: The key finding is that mixing multiple generators' arXiv outputs still yields K*=3 with 2v3 AUC=0.6436. This strengthens the "domain-determined K*" claim. I should add this to the appendix, perhaps to the existing multi-source section. Let me check if there's already a multi-source section.

Looking at the registry, there's also `exp_code_multi_source` for Code domain multi-source. The paper mentions individual model experiments on CodeSearchNet Python in §4.5, where all four models yield K*=2, but that's different from multi-source experiments where a single classifier trains on mixed data from multiple generators. The arxiv_multi_source result shows that even with mixed arXiv text from different sources, the classifier still converges to K*=3, which is a stronger claim about domain-level constraints.

For `wiki_domain_llama`: LLaMA-8B on Wiki with K*=1 is already covered in the bootstrap table at line 259, so no changes needed there. Let me add the arxiv_multi_source result to the appendix as additional validation of the domain-determined K* hypothesis.

Now I should check if there are any other experiments still running that might finish soon.

```text
68	\subsection{Additional Scope Analysis}
69	\label{sec:extended_limitations}
70	
71	\emph{Prompt-chain versus retrain-chain.}
72	Retrain-chain validation (Pythia-1.4B LoRA, 5 generations) yields $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.9484$, 2v3 $= 0.8888$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$), far exceeding prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$.
73	The elevated signal arises from cumulative model-adaptation artifacts rather than text-intrinsic degradation; prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ is the operationally relevant boundary for data curation.
74	
75	\emph{Threshold sensitivity.}
76	At $\theta = 0.55$, $\Kempirical = 3$ for Qwen-rewrite/continuation; at $\theta = 0.70$, $\Kempirical = 1$ across all configurations (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
77	Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.2\,pp; all ten boundary comparisons yield permutation $p < 0.001$.
78	
79	\emph{Structural confound: token length.}
80	In continuation mode the generation prompt is discarded, so all depths $\geq 1$ consist entirely of machine-generated tokens and the 1v2 boundary compares texts differing only in conditioning context (human-authored vs.\ machine-generated prompt), not structural composition.
81	Depth~0 texts average ${\sim}$191 subword tokens (C4 truncation), while depth~1+ texts average 193--256 tokens depending on model (Table~\ref{tab:token_length}).
82	This asymmetry is directionally opposite to a length-inflation confound: shorter depth-0 texts provide less feature-extraction context, making 0v1 discrimination harder, not easier.
83	Within depths 1--5, mean token counts are stable (within-model range $\leq$ 4 tokens for all configurations except Qwen-continuation at 6 tokens), so the pairwise comparisons underlying $\Kempirical{=}2$ are unaffected by length.
84	EOS truncation rates are similarly depth-stable within each model: Qwen-rewrite 7--11\%, Pythia 34--36\%, OLMo $<$1\%, Qwen-continuation 17--20\%, Qwen-7B 45--47\%.
85	Models with the highest truncation (Pythia, Qwen-7B) still yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ with the same step-function AUC decay pattern as low-truncation models (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}), confirming that EOS truncation does not drive the boundary.
86	
87	\emph{Graduated filtering.}
88	All $\alpha$ values (0.1--0.9) underperform binary-ours on MMLU ($\alpha{=}0.1$: 0.5873; $\alpha{=}0.9$: 0.5892 vs.\ 0.5941); at $\alpha{=}0.9$, HellaSwag matches exactly (0.6701).
89	Binary-ours significantly outperforms all alternatives on MMLU ($p_{\text{corr}} \leq 0.021$); Graduated$_{0.9}$ is indistinguishable on HellaSwag ($p = 1.00$) but worse on MMLU ($p_{\text{corr}} = 0.011$).
90	\label{tab:pairwise_significance}
91	
92	\subsection{Dose-Response: Contamination Ratio Effect}
93	\label{sec:dose_response}
94	
95	The main filtering experiments (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) use 100\% synthetic data to maximize effect size.
96	To characterize behavior at lower contamination ratios, we mix synthetic depths 1--5 with clean C4 at 10\%, 30\%, and 50\% contamination (Qwen-1.5B continuation, 5 seeds each; Table~\ref{tab:dose_response}).
97	
```

      "content": "\\subsection{Additional Scope Analysis}\n\\label{sec:extended_limitations}\n\n\\emph{Prompt-chain versus retrain-chain.}\nRetrain-chain validation (Pythia-1.4B LoRA, 5 generations) yields $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.9484$, 2v3 $= 0.8888$, 3v4 $= 0.7982$), far exceeding prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$.\nThe elevated signal arises from cumulative model-adaptation artifacts rather than text-intrinsic degradation; prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ is the operationally relevant boundary for data curation.\n\n\\emph{Threshold sensitivity.}\nAt $\\theta = 0.55$, $\\Kempirical = 3$ for Qwen-rewrite/continuation; at $\\theta = 0.70$, $\\Kempirical = 1$ across all configurations (Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nQwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.2\\,pp; all ten boundary comparisons yield permutation $p < 0.001$.\n\n\\emph{Structural confound: token length.}\nIn continuation mode the generation prompt is discarded, so all depths $\\geq 1$ consist entirely of machine-generated tokens and the 1v2 boundary compares texts differing only in conditioning context (human-authored vs.\\ machine-generated prompt), not structural composition.\nDepth~0 texts average ${\\sim}$191 subword tokens (C4 truncation), while depth~1+ texts average 193--256 tokens depending on model (Table~\\ref{tab:token_length}).\nThis asymmetry is directionally opposite to a length-inflation confound: shorter depth-0 texts provide less feature-extraction context, making 0v1 discrimination harder, not easier.\nWithin depths 1--5, mean token counts are stable (within-model range $\\leq$ 4 tokens for all configurations except Qwen-continuation at 6 tokens), so the pairwise comparisons underlying $\\Kempirical{=}2$ are unaffected by length.\nEOS truncation rates are similarly depth-stable within each model: Qwen-rewrite 7--11\\%, Pythia 34--36\\%, OLMo $<$1\\%, Qwen-continuation 17--20\\%, Qwen-7B 45--47\\%.\nModels with the highest truncation (Pythia, Qwen-7B) still yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with the same step-function AUC decay pattern as low-truncation models (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}), confirming that EOS truncation does not drive the boundary.\n\n\\emph{Graduated filtering.}\nAll $\\alpha$ values (0.1--0.9) underperform binary-ours on MMLU ($\\alpha{=}0.1$: 0.5873; $\\alpha{=}0.9$: 0.5892 vs.\\ 0.5941); at $\\alpha{=}0.9$, HellaSwag matches exactly (0.6701).\nBinary-ours significantly outperforms all alternatives on MMLU ($p_{\\text{corr}} \\leq 0.021$); Graduated$_{0.9}$ is indistinguishable on HellaSwag ($p = 1.00$) but worse on MMLU ($p_{\\text{corr}} = 0.011$).\n\\label{tab:pairwise_significance}\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response: Contamination Ratio Effect}\n\\label{sec:dose_response}\n\nThe main filtering experiments (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) use 100\\% synthetic data to maximize effect size.\nTo characterize behavior at lower contamination ratios, we mix synthetic depths 1--5 with clean C4 at 10\\%, 30\\%, and 50\\% contamination (Qwen-1.5B continuation, 5 seeds each; Table~\\ref{tab:dose_response}).\n",

    }

349	This confirms that depth features are text-intrinsic and scale-modulated rather than model-specific artifacts.
